It’s more like things feel flat and harder to get going. I haven’t really noticed myself feeling down nearly every day, at least not in that clear way.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling more fatigued or low on energy lately, even when you haven’t done anything physically or mentally taxing?\n\nPatient: Yeah, kind of. It’s not like I’m physically exhausted exactly, but things feel heavier than they should. The photography walks, mentoring, even just keeping my calendar organized—I’m not starting them the way I normally would. It feels flat, and I end up putting it off.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way, with things feeling flat and harder to get started?\n\nPatient: It’s been going on for about a year and three months. It started after a family member’s health and behavior changed, and I ended up taking on a lot more coordination around that. Since then, things have just felt flatter and harder to start.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced days where your mood feels low or empty, not just a lack of interest in doing things, but a noticeable sadness or emptiness that sticks with you for most of the day?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I mean, I have off days where I feel kind of blah, but I wouldn’t say there’s a noticeable sadness or emptiness that stays with me most of the day. It’s more just feeling flat.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your ability to think clearly, concentrate, or make decisions lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah, a little. I can still do my job overall, but I’ve noticed I miss small calendar details more than I used to. I have to double-check things that used to feel automatic. And outside of work, I’ve pulled back from photography outings and mentoring some, because I don’t feel as present or as reliable as I should.\n\nDoctor: Have you found yourself feeling more guilty or like you’re not worth as much as you used to, even when you haven’t done anything wrong or out of the ordinary?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think so. I get frustrated with myself when I drop the ball on little things, but I wouldn’t say I feel worthless or unusually guilty. It’s more annoyance than anything.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel down or depressed most of the day, nearly every day, even when nothing particularly bad is happening?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn’t say that. I’m not feeling down or depressed most of the day nearly every day. I have moments where I feel off or kind of flat, but not a steady depressed mood when nothing’s going on.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that activities you used to enjoy, like photography walks or mentoring the kids, no longer feel pleasurable or rewarding, even when you do manage to start them?\n\nPatient: Yeah. That’s actually a big part of why I came in. The stuff that usually makes me feel like myself—photography walks, mentoring the kids, even just staying on top of my calendar—started feeling kind of flat and heavy. Even when I do them, they don’t feel as rewarding, and I’ve stopped starting them on my own as much.\n\nDoctor: When you do manage to go on a photography walk or meet with the kids you mentor, do you find that you feel less pleasure or reward from those activities than you used to, even while you're doing them?\n\nPatient: Yes. Even when I get myself to go, it feels kind of flat. Like I’m doing the photography walk or meeting with the kids, but it doesn’t give me the same lift or reward it used to. It feels heavier, and I’m not initiating those things like I normally would.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight or appetite lately, like losing or gaining weight without trying?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I haven’t noticed any major weight change, and my appetite seems about the same.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your sleep, like waking up much earlier than usual and not being able to fall back asleep, or having trouble falling or staying asleep?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I have. Some nights I’ll only sleep maybe two or three hours, then I’m up before dawn and I don’t feel tired. I’ll feel ready to start answering emails or updating appointment lists like it’s normal working time.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. I haven’t really noticed myself feeling down nearly every day, at least not in that clear way.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling more fatigued or low on energy lately, even when you haven’t done anything physically or mentally taxing?\n\nPatient: Yeah, kind of.
